# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.
---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

 ## 0. Summary
@@ -2564,3 +2564,48 @@ recipe names separate token revocation and existing-grant cleanup obligations; s
 Population conditions likewise remain exact materialised probes rather than
 unbounded rule scans. The recipe book records the application obligation and
 privacy tests; it is not a claim of new sync audiences or analyzer reach.
+
+## 22. Wave 12 authorization recipes and open language decisions (RULES2)
+
+The rule-recipes card compiles and executes day/window roster access,
+column-level privacy via public identity/private sheet tables, wider status
+counts and readable role queries, mutable-parent audiences, minimum-group
+publication, secret ballots with closed publication, expiring media capability
+grants, enrolment-scoped ephemeral presence and system-job blind comparisons.
+Invite, self-join and public metadata are separate enrolment models. Trusted
+writers maintain a materialised publication in the same commit as its deciding
+state; a separate repair job cannot be the revocation boundary.
+
+Four decisions remain open; their end games and current disposition are:
+
+- **Column-level read rules:** a field has its own declared authority, with
+  unavailable fields represented explicitly in generated results, indexing,
+  aggregates, downloads and device projections. Do not implement here: masking
+  a shape is insufficient, and changing only serialization leaks through filters
+  and derived data. Public identity/private sheet is the current row-authority
+  recipe, not the primitive. Field-write authority is independent.
+- **Roster plus one group sync union:** deliver both audiences with arm-tagged
+  scope identities, independently revoke one arm while preserving another,
+  acquire both arms with epoch-safe snapshots and prove coverage on both stores.
+  Do not relax the checker here: the empty-prefix roster is already the one
+  group arm and current R2 custody cannot represent a second. Merge roles into
+  one roster or remain online. LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8 owns this protocol work.
+- **Windowed union horizons:** per-arm acquired floors and coverage certificates
+  survive moves, expiry, leave/rejoin, snapshots and pagination without emitting
+  never-authorized IDs. Do not implement here: the prior expiry/sweep ID-disclosure
+  counterexample caused the explicit narrowing; all relevant device/simulator
+  and differential traces must precede reopening LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8.9.
+  Query pagination remains supported; it is not an acquisition window.
+- **changed-only(field):** schema-aware transition sugar expands to equality
+  of every other writable field between old and next, with a documented policy
+  for added columns, field-owned writes and system-maintained fields. Do not
+  add syntax here: the expansion's evolving-schema policy is the unresolved
+  part, not parsing. Explicit old/next equalities are sound today. Language
+  design belongs with the rule grammar, not a permissive mutation require.
+
+Diagnostic contradiction notes are specified in LLP 3000.036.002 §9. The
+refusals ladder shows client codes per stage while preserving authored statement
+order; a nullable hidden lookup is null, an asserted hidden lookup uses its
+assertion code (conventionally NOT_FOUND), and a readable denied write is E_RULE.
+Transport authentication guidance and the rest of client text belong to SURF;
+proof-kit/testing authoring belongs to KIT, except the hidden-only page pointer.

 ## 1. Scope and motivation
@@ -283,3 +283,54 @@ unchanged hidden-row envelopes, and fixed-seed campaigns with no new failure
 signatures. The census and oracle summaries are in
 `snapback4/reports/wave11-qdiag/`; WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md records the final checks
 and independent root Snapback 1 environment preconditions.
+
+## 9. Wave 12 table-rule composition and dead nullable lookups (RULES2)
+
+This amendment adds advisory contradiction proofs, not authority or a new
+admission gate. N_WRITE_DENIED covers an update or the update arm of an upsert
+whose rule contradicts the preceding mutation require/branch conditions.
+N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE covers a nullable unique point lookup whose read rule
+contradicts those conditions: null is the only result and its non-null branch
+cannot execute. A failed asserted lookup remains governed by the existing
+refusal-order notes. Jobs are exempt.
+
+The proof fragment is equality/inequality of viewer, typed literals, scalar
+aliases, uniquely addressed row fields and old/next images, with boolean
+composition. Unnamed update rules must hold for both images; named rules bind
+the images explicitly. Unknown expressions are overapproximations. Program
+probes are caller-filtered reads and cannot be equated with table-rule probes;
+absence of a role check never proves absence of that role. Different owners,
+roles and memberships can coincide. Enumerate at most 64 alternatives; larger
+formulas prove nothing. Only a satisfiable gate with an unsatisfiable
+intersection emits a note. This deliberately makes no whole-language completeness
+claim. Read-through, maintained lookups, field-owned patches, spreads and
+ambiguous keys stay quiet.
+
+Facts are local to statement order and branch paths. Clear all row/scalar
+snapshot evidence after a write or opaque call, before compound effects,
+after branches/rebinding and across loop/viewer scopes. Requirements after a
+write cannot justify a note on that earlier write. Follow no opaque helpers:
+this is a safe underapproximation of note reach, never a guess about effects.
+Notes use source schema facts only; they add no hidden-row wire information.
+
+Qualification: compiled guide snippets and engine execution test every recipe;
+positive contradiction and negative overlap/unknown/invalidation witnesses test
+the notes. Run check on read-only copies of all 100 wave-12 final schemas
+before and after, report deltas by code and inspect each new firing before
+claiming precision. No runtime, planner, sync, expiry, protocol or adoption
+semantics change; existing simulator/device/difftest suites verify that boundary.
+Fresh mutually blind Muse and Codex reviews cover this amendment and the
+recipe/open-decision amendment in LLP 3000.036 §22.
+
+Nullable lookup snapshots supply no physical field facts; only asserted
+successful lookups supply them. A uniformly denied page is complete and empty;
+a nonuniform scan can remain partial even when it returns no visible rows.
+The testing pointer names this distinction instead of falsely promising that
+physical exhaustion certifies every partly readable population. This is the
+existing completeness law, not a new planner behavior.
+
+Numeric literal terms use the binder's canonical integer value, not token
+spelling (`01` and `1` are equal). Calls/writes in an if condition invalidate
+entry evidence before either branch; branch-body effects alone do not erase
+otherwise valid entry proofs. Regression witnesses cover an effectful condition
+that changes owner before an admitted body update, and equal numeric spellings.

     1	//! Proved contradictions between caller gates and table rules; unknown is quiet.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — bounded, path-local contradiction proofs never infer that different roles or principals are disjoint.
     3	use super::catalog::Catalog;
     4	use crate::{Diagnostic, ast::*};
     5	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     6	
     7	type Atom = (String, String, bool); // equality when true, inequality otherwise
     8	// DNF: no alternatives is false; one empty alternative is unknown/true.
     9	type Facts = Vec<Vec<Atom>>;
    10	const LIMIT: usize = 64;
    11	fn unknown() -> Facts {
    12	    vec![vec![]]
    13	}
    14	fn combine(a: Facts, b: Facts, and: bool) -> Facts {
    15	    if !and {
    16	        return if a.len() + b.len() > LIMIT {
    17	            unknown()
    18	        } else {
    19	            a.into_iter().chain(b).collect()
    20	        };
    21	    }
    22	    if a.len().saturating_mul(b.len()) > LIMIT {
    23	        return unknown();
    24	    }
    25	    a.into_iter()
    26	        .flat_map(|a| b.iter().map(move |b| a.iter().chain(b).cloned().collect()))
    27	        .collect()
    28	}
    29	fn possible(facts: &Facts) -> bool {
    30	    facts.iter().any(|facts| {
    31	        let mut roots = BTreeMap::<String, String>::new();
    32	        fn root(roots: &BTreeMap<String, String>, term: &str) -> String {
    33	            let mut at = term;
    34	            while let Some(next) = roots.get(at) {
    35	                at = next;
    36	            }
    37	            at.into()
    38	        }
    39	        for (a, b, equal) in facts {
    40	            if !equal {
    41	                continue;
    42	            }
    43	            let (a, b) = (root(&roots, a), root(&roots, b));
    44	            if a != b {
    45	                roots.insert(a, b);
    46	            }
    47	        }
    48	        let mut literals = BTreeMap::new();
    49	        for (a, b, _) in facts {
    50	            for term in [a, b] {
    51	                if term.starts_with("literal:") {
    52	                    let r = root(&roots, term);
    53	                    if let Some(old) = literals.insert(r, term)
    54	                        && old != term
    55	                    {
    56	                        return false;
    57	                    }
    58	                }
    59	            }
    60	        }
    61	        facts
    62	            .iter()
    63	            .all(|(a, b, eq)| *eq || root(&roots, a) != root(&roots, b))
    64	    })
    65	}
    66	#[derive(Clone, Default)]
    67	struct Env {
    68	    references: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    69	    values: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    70	    rows: BTreeMap<String, (String, BTreeMap<String, String>)>,
    71	}
    72	fn term(expr: &Expr, env: &Env, row: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>) -> Option<String> {
    73	    match expr {
    74	        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Some("viewer".into()),
    75	        Expr::Name { name, .. } => env
    76	            .values
    77	            .get(name)
    78	            .cloned()
    79	            .or_else(|| env.rows.get(name)?.1.get("id").cloned()),
    80	        Expr::Literal {
    81	            value: Literal::Number(text),
    82	            site,
    83	        } => Some(format!(
    84	            "literal:Number({})",
    85	            super::integer_literal(text, site).ok()?
    86	        )),
    87	        Expr::Literal { value, .. } => Some(format!("literal:{value:?}")),
    88	        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => match base.as_ref() {
    89	            Expr::Focal { .. } => row?.get(name).cloned(),
    90	            Expr::Name { name: alias, .. } => env.rows.get(alias)?.1.get(name).cloned(),
    91	            _ => None,
    92	        },
    93	        _ => None,
    94	    }
    95	}
    96	fn predicate(
    97	    expr: &Expr,
    98	    env: &Env,
    99	    row: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   100	    positive: bool,
   101	    table_rule: bool,
   102	) -> Facts {
   103	    match expr {
   104	        Expr::Name { name, .. }
   105	            if ["true", "false"].contains(&name.as_str())
   106	                || table_rule && ["allow", "deny"].contains(&name.as_str()) =>
   107	        {
   108	            if (["allow", "true"].contains(&name.as_str())) == positive {
   109	                unknown()
   110	            } else {
   111	                vec![]
   112	            }
   113	        }
   114	        Expr::Literal {
   115	            value: Literal::Bool(value),
   116	            ..
   117	        } => {
   118	            if *value == positive {
   119	                unknown()
   120	            } else {
   121	                vec![]
   122	            }
   123	        }
   124	        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => {
   125	            predicate(value, env, row, !positive, table_rule)
   126	        }
   127	        Expr::Binary {
   128	            op, left, right, ..
   129	        } if op == "and" || op == "or" => combine(
   130	            predicate(left, env, row, positive, table_rule),
   131	            predicate(right, env, row, positive, table_rule),
   132	            (op == "and") == positive,
   133	        ),
   134	        Expr::Binary {
   135	            op, left, right, ..
   136	        } if op == "=" || op == "!=" => match (term(left, env, row), term(right, env, row)) {
   137	            (Some(a), Some(b)) => vec![vec![(a, b, (op == "=") == positive)]],
   138	            _ => unknown(),
   139	        },
   140	        // Program probes are reads, whereas rule probes see hidden rows. Never
   141	        // equate them or assume unrelated roster rows cannot overlap.
   142	        _ => unknown(),
   143	    }
   144	}
   145	fn row(
   146	    catalog: &Catalog,

   215	pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
   216	    let mut out = vec![];
   217	    for p in catalog
   218	        .documents
   219	        .iter()
   220	        .flat_map(|d| &d.programs)
   221	        .filter(|p| p.kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && !p.native)

   232	        visit(catalog, p, &body, env, unknown(), &mut out);
   233	    }

   235	}
   236	fn visit(
   237	    catalog: &Catalog,
   238	    p: &ProgramDecl,
   239	    body: &[Stmt],
   240	    mut env: Env,
   241	    mut facts: Facts,
   242	    out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   243	) {
   244	    for s in body {
   245	        match s {
   246	            Stmt::If {
   247	                condition,
   248	                then_body,
   249	                else_body,
   250	                ..
   251	            } => {
   252	                // A condition helper can write before either branch. Analyze
   253	                // just the condition, not the branch bodies, for entry effects.
   254	                let condition_stmt = Stmt::Expr {
   255	                    value: condition.clone(),
   256	                    site: condition.site().clone(),
   257	                };
   258	                if expressions(&condition_stmt)
   259	                    .iter()
   260	                    .any(|e| matches!(e, Expr::Write { .. } | Expr::Call { .. }))
   261	                {
   262	                    env = Env::default();
   263	                    facts = unknown();
   264	                }
   265	                visit(
   266	                    catalog,
   267	                    p,
   268	                    then_body,
   269	                    env.clone(),
   270	                    combine(
   271	                        facts.clone(),
   272	                        predicate(condition, &env, None, true, false),
   273	                        true,
   274	                    ),
   275	                    out,
   276	                );
   277	                visit(
   278	                    catalog,
   279	                    p,
   280	                    else_body,
   281	                    env.clone(),
   282	                    combine(
   283	                        facts.clone(),
   284	                        predicate(condition, &env, None, false, false),
   285	                        true,
   286	                    ),
   287	                    out,
   288	                );
   289	                // Branch writes/rebindings may invalidate every entry fact.
   290	                env = Env::default();
   291	                facts = unknown();
   292	                continue;
   293	            }
   294	            Stmt::For { body, .. } => {
   295	                visit(catalog, p, body, Env::default(), unknown(), out);
   296	                env = Env::default();
   297	                facts = unknown();
   298	                continue;
   299	            }
   300	            Stmt::As { .. } => {
   301	                env = Env::default();
   302	                facts = unknown();
   303	                continue;
   304	            }
   305	            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => break,
   306	            _ => {}
   307	        }
   308	        let es = expressions(s);
   309	        // Calls and compound effects are opaque; drop stale facts before any
   310	        // expression that could change a witnessed row, including helper calls.
   311	        let effects = es
   312	            .iter()
   313	            .filter(|e| matches!(e, Expr::Write { .. } | Expr::Call { .. }))
   314	            .count();
   315	        if effects > 0
   316	            && !(effects == 1
   317	                && matches!(
   318	                    s,
   319	                    Stmt::Expr {
   320	                        value: Expr::Write { .. },
   321	                        ..
   322	                    } | Stmt::Bind {
   323	                        value: Expr::Write { .. },
   324	                        ..
   325	                    } | Stmt::Return {
   326	                        value: Expr::Write { .. },
   327	                        ..
   328	                    }
   329	                ))
   330	        {
   331	            facts = unknown();
   332	            env = Env::default();
   333	        }
   334	        for e in &es {
   335	            if let Expr::Write {
   336	                op: WriteOp::Update | WriteOp::Upsert,
   337	                table,
   338	                keys,
   339	                fields,
   340	                site,
   341	                ..
   342	            } = e
   343	            {
   344	                let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(table) else {
   345	                    continue;
   346	                };

## 22. Wave 12 authorization recipes and open language decisions (RULES2)

The rule-recipes card compiles and executes day/window roster access,
column-level privacy via public identity/private sheet tables, wider status
counts and readable role queries, mutable-parent audiences, minimum-group
publication, secret ballots with closed publication, expiring media capability
grants, enrolment-scoped ephemeral presence and system-job blind comparisons.
Invite, self-join and public metadata are separate enrolment models. Trusted
writers maintain a materialised publication in the same commit as its deciding
state; a separate repair job cannot be the revocation boundary.

Four decisions remain open; their end games and current disposition are:

- **Column-level read rules:** a field has its own declared authority, with
  unavailable fields represented explicitly in generated results, indexing,
  aggregates, downloads and device projections. Do not implement here: masking
  a shape is insufficient, and changing only serialization leaks through filters
  and derived data. Public identity/private sheet is the current row-authority
  recipe, not the primitive. Field-write authority is independent.
- **Roster plus one group sync union:** deliver both audiences with arm-tagged
  scope identities, independently revoke one arm while preserving another,
  acquire both arms with epoch-safe snapshots and prove coverage on both stores.
  Do not relax the checker here: the empty-prefix roster is already the one
  group arm and current R2 custody cannot represent a second. Merge roles into
  one roster or remain online. LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8 owns this protocol work.
- **Windowed union horizons:** per-arm acquired floors and coverage certificates
  survive moves, expiry, leave/rejoin, snapshots and pagination without emitting
  never-authorized IDs. Do not implement here: the prior expiry/sweep ID-disclosure
  counterexample caused the explicit narrowing; all relevant device/simulator
  and differential traces must precede reopening LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8.9.
  Query pagination remains supported; it is not an acquisition window.
- **changed-only(field):** schema-aware transition sugar expands to equality
  of every other writable field between old and next, with a documented policy
  for added columns, field-owned writes and system-maintained fields. Do not
  add syntax here: the expansion's evolving-schema policy is the unresolved
  part, not parsing. Explicit old/next equalities are sound today. Language
  design belongs with the rule grammar, not a permissive mutation require.

Diagnostic contradiction notes are specified in LLP 3000.036.002 §9. The
refusals ladder shows client codes per stage while preserving authored statement
order; a nullable hidden lookup is null, an asserted hidden lookup uses its
assertion code (conventionally NOT_FOUND), and a readable denied write is E_RULE.
Transport authentication guidance and the rest of client text belong to SURF;
proof-kit/testing authoring belongs to KIT, except the hidden-only page pointer.
# LLP 3000.036.002: Intent-aware lints and production bootstrap

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language diagnostics, CLI fixtures and production readiness, core audience-grant validation and development refusal traces
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (DIAG)
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Revised:** 2026-10-14 (RULES2 wave 12 amendment); 2026-10-14 (QDIAG amendment §§7–8; original implementation 2026-10-13)
**Related:** [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.036.000](./3000.036.000-sharing-shapes-at-sync-and-union-audiences.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) §20.6, [LLP 3000.011.003.000](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md) §8

## 1. Scope and motivation

Wave 10 fix-list #5, #7 and #15 expose disagreement between diagnostics and
execution: deliberate negative fixture tests look stale, one missing roster
produces many writer notes, and the recommended service-seat bootstrap fails
membership-grant validation. The coordinator authorizes this work and its
reviews. HS owns handshake/types, REL2 release lifecycle, DX2 starters/client
recipes, and PAGE2 general planner/rules expressiveness.

## 2. Service seats grant membership on the server

An entire positive branch `viewer = 'service:NAME'` with a nonempty name
satisfies the membership insert/update grant guard. The namespace is reserved:
accounts cannot mint it, and the operator mints a seat token against the
production store. This authority may enroll the first member or retarget a
mutable membership. An `and` branch containing that equality is likewise
restricted to the seat. An `or` requires every branch independently to prove
membership, a newly created group, or a seat. Negation, inequality, arbitrary
principal literals, and ordinary self-join remain insufficient.

Read-rule service grants already disappear before device audience inference
(LLP 3000.007 §20.6); this change adds no sync audience, capability or wire
format. Devices never hold service-seat partitions. Existing seat-session expiry and
revocation checks apply before a mutation is dispatched; no token lifecycle
changes are proposed. Only mintable service names (1–64 ASCII alphanumeric,
underscore or hyphen) qualify for production readiness; an impossible seat
keeps the root's N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER note rather than counting as a writer. Adding membership still
uses ordinary constraints, transactional logs and scope delivery/revocation.
An authenticated seat writes via an ordinary mutation, without system bypass.
The deploy card compiles a synced staff-roster example. The diagnostic's
inline recipe retains an already-online roster's residency and its entire
read predicate; references are resolved in the application's existing schema. Refused ordinary
self-join offers that trusted bootstrap beside the prior-member alternative,
without a redundant `(service and member) or member` rewrite. The offered
seat rule replaces the unsafe branch, never adds a seat to an otherwise
unprotected self-join.

## 3. Readiness notes identify blockers

Keep the positive-dependency least fixed point of LLP 3000.011.003.000 §8.
For blocked tables, dependencies are positive insert-rule probes/reference
terms and a read-through parent. Group mutually dependent tables; report one
note per root blocked component, listing the blocked descendants it governs.
Multiple roots can name a shared dependent. A table with no writer is a root
blocker even if its rule mentions another blocked table. Optional alternatives
remain conservative: this is advice, not a satisfiability/reachability theorem.
Native opaque jobs still prevent an absence claim. A persona-only schema
(with no production identity method) emits one production-identity note,
including the blocked tables when any exist, rather than one per table.

## 4. Fixture intent and guard visibility

`check` inspects shared cases only for operations declared by the checked
schema. Signature/decoding/validation failures whose runtime code is E_INPUT
are intentional when `expect` equals E_INPUT, so emit no argument-drift note.
Malformed JSON/case metadata remain authoring errors. Unknown operations
remain a hard error in `test`, including misspellings; read-only `check`
intentionally cannot distinguish shared newer operations from misspellings.
Expectations are the existing public refusal code, without a new phase field;
the runner prints the E_INPUT message/subcause on each passing negative case
so a negative assertion is reviewable without introducing another note. Saved runtime values
remain unknown. The fixture runner recognizes decoding/signature errors as
E_INPUT too; tests cannot claim success by skipping the case.

A program `exists` sees only caller-readable rows; a table-rule probe has
authority over all rows. Keep those semantics. Add N_UNREADABLE_PROBE on
negative requirements and conditional guards over private populations unless
the pins/preceding guards prove readability. Positive `require exists` is
itself an authorization check; retain its existing N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN analysis.
To cover values later used through aliases/returned helper booleans, inspect
point-existence observations when they happen, as well as mutation/job prefix
observations. Pure query prefix/search/seek values remain ordinary visible-hit
answers; trace guard-consumed bindings through aliases/objects/possible rebinding
and check those observations with their original authority. Inputs to a
guard-consuming helper/shape are conservatively tracked too (all inputs,
rather than an unsound partial parameter-use proof), including direct expression
inputs and guarded loop domains. Row-dependent candidate filters remain intentional
visible-hit query predicates (global gates lower to whole-page conditionals
and are checked); a computed boolean field passed to a guard is
still an observation. Snapshot markers exclude names in every actual argument,
so postorder substitution cannot match a fabricated name in another argument.
Helpers/shapes reached from a requirement inspect every direct input value,
including helpers that only invert it in their returned expression. A direct
positive `require exists` remains authorization. Traverse requirement
wrappers (members, objects, calls and nested conditionals), preserving direct
positive-existence authorization. Inline shape bases are inspected independently
of named-definition discovery. Expand object/write shorthand in analysis copies
before classifying global facts, discovering effects, checking shadowing, or
excluding/substituting/validating argument names; shorthand and explicit names
must have the same scope and proof behavior. Runtime expressions stay unchanged.
Name the target read
rule and explain that negative guards cannot enforce global absence. This is
advisory; an intentionally caller-visible guard can remain unchanged.

Follow reachable helpers, named shapes and spreads. Facts cross helper scopes
only through actual arguments or viewer/literals, with fresh intermediate names
to prevent caller/formal spelling collisions. A read-only helper can receive
snapshot evidence for an expression argument; a writing helper gets only scalar
names. Successful ruled physical-row lookups prove their unambiguous indexed
row exists, omitting extra projected-field tests. Viewer has one meaning in
user text and compiler-generated recipes. Every extra read-rule equality must
resolve; none may be dropped. Never borrow a positional index's visibility
proof from another same-width index. An explicit computed/unknown index never
falls back to another index, and computed keys are not raw column pins.

Dominating facts are invalidated by writes, relevant binding changes or viewer
scope changes; an unanalysed control-flow path never proves authority. Follow
helper/shape effects conservatively through the call graph; cycles prove
nothing. Potentially writing loops cannot carry entry facts across backedges
or export prior role evidence. Binding-only loops invalidate potentially rebound
names at entry/exit, including break/continue paths. An in-loop readable guard
can reacquire proof each iteration. Inspect domain conditionals too. Before
inspecting a compound expression that may write, drop prior facts because a
later operand can observe the changed world. A standalone helper call with
non-writing arguments instead analyzes the helper's statements in order, then
drops caller evidence if it wrote. Stop at return/break/continue. Viewer scopes
inside jobs use caller reads and receive the note; surrounding system job
guards remain exempt. Use a unique/cap/final constraint for an invariant across
hidden rows; do not recommend making private rows public. Avoid duplicate notes
at one guard/target. This never changes execution or admission.

## 5. Refusals and usable rewrites

Invalid mixed positional/named bracket keys get a specific diagnostic before the
misleading id-type error. Name both consistent spellings, using source text
and the resolved key where available. For `!=` inside a probe, explain that
bracket tests are named equalities; use a pinned key with an external predicate
or a bounded program filter. Table rules remain unique point probes. Moving a test to a program filter
changes visibility to caller-readable rows; explain this explicitly.

A nullable upsert result must be checked with `! else CODE` before it supplies
a reference; `! else` refuses and rolls back the whole mutation when null; explain that the write may succeed while its resulting row is
unreadable. Keep upsert result semantics unchanged.

E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT offers a point read by id and a publicly authorized
maintained aggregate beside the existing scoped scan/constraint/job choices.
E_JOB_CURSOR_INDEX leads with stable id order. E_SYNC never repeats an online
only edit already applied; for an inherited read-through conflict it names the
parent audience and copied-column alternative. A union parent ignores read-through children already marked `online only`
when finding a child that inherits its device audience; it names a synced child
and the copied-column alternative. This implements the existing online-only
repair without adding union capture/retention support. Every emitted concrete rewrite
is compiled against its input schema with zero errors, extending the wave-9
golden test. Advisory notes are permitted when the unchanged schema itself
already carries them; snippet guide blocks must remain note-free.

Fixture loading is operator-owned system work: preserve actual constraint
refusals, including named unique codes, rather than remapping to NOT_FOUND.
Attach table/constraint context in fixture-loader text. The same preservation applies to system-job upsert inserts: a system actor
has no hidden target, so its malformed image reports its constraint instead
of NOT_FOUND. Ordinary callers retain hidden-target/collision suppression. Readable unique conflicts return the
key's authored code when readable under the current caller at write time,
in the same transaction; hidden conflicts keep their non-disclosing answer.
Development traces name a failed read-through parent and viewer-hidden lookups
with the read-rule source. Public client refusals never disclose a hidden row
or the private clause. Do not introduce a public E_RULE variant that reveals
whether the parent exists.

## 6. Acceptance and review

Affected tests cover E_INPUT negative cases, older schemas/shared fixtures,
root/cycle readiness grouping, persona collapse, service bootstrap and sync
revocation, rejected untrusted grants, private guard notes and quiet proven
probes, mixed probes, upsert narrowing, inequality guidance, compiled rewrites,
fixture constraint rollback, readable/hidden unique collisions, and private
parent/read-rule evidence. Run affected Rust/guide suites and ref-check; never
the full scripts/test.sh. Save fresh Muse and Codex reviews verbatim under
llp/reviews and revise until both mark the same text READY.

## 7. Wave 11 diagnostic precision (QDIAG)

The nullable-reference lint counts a null value as needing no target lookup.
At `if arg != null`, only the null edge carries that exemption; a lookup on
the other edge must still dominate the write. Join by intersection and clear
facts on rebinding. This changes advisory output only.

Operation parameter defaults remain explicit: declare an optional parameter
and bind `value = arg ?? literal`. Refuse `= literal` with that exact idiom.
A multi-line `require` keeps `else CODE` on its closing line; the refusal names
that line and preserves the author's code. `?` precedes `on delete keep`.

Program absence checks on a row lookup (`t[key] = null`) use the unreadable
probe note too. Raw table-rule probes, including delete rules, still observe
all rows; the note's repair states that distinction. A later owner `require`
after `! else NOT_FOUND` gets a refusal-order note only when every read-rule
branch requires that same owner. A branch admitting staff or another party
can legitimately reach the custom refusal and keeps the note quiet.

A child-owner proof requires equality in every child insert branch, immutable
(or unupdatable) child owner/reference and parent owner, no maintained or
field-owned value, no opaque/job writer, no parent expiry (expiry fires no reference action), and no kept parent identity
that ordinary or cascading deletion could recreate,
and equal read rules after replacing
only that owner. No other row fields may appear in either rule. A successful
parent lookup (or a direct probe in its returned row projection) supplies the
parent-readable fact. This is advisory analysis of ordinary admitted writes;
trusted system seeds must preserve the declared application invariant. It never
changes read authority, admission or completeness.

## 8. Wave 11 contextual spellings and refusal codes (QDIAG amendment)

A required same-table row in a reference-typed column, point key, or operation
argument lowers to its `.id`. This includes an inserted snapshot and a looked-up
snapshot; it evaluates its expression once in source order, introduces no
lookup, and grants no existence or read authority. Shorthand fields get the same
projection. Cross-table rows and optional rows remain refused; an optional
upsert must be narrowed with `! else CODE` first. A generic `id` or `principal`
position does not implicitly project a row. Helper parameters retain their
existing call-site row specialization (LLP 3000.007 §5.2; the existing helper implementation specializes same-table rows at the call site): a helper called with
a row keeps its row fields, and one called with a reference must look it up.
Lists and objects receive no deep automatic projection. Explicit `.id` remains
valid. Generated API and runtime IR remain those of the explicit spelling.

Image and video bounds accept integer decimal byte units KB, MB and GB; 5MB
means 5,000,000 bytes. Overflow above u32 refuses at parsing. This is a constant
spelling, not a new value type, upload format, meter or protocol capability.

Readable secondary unique collisions with no authored `else CODE` now return
E_UNIQUE and name the declared key columns, never an internal planner index.
Authored codes still win. The intrinsic id conflict remains E_CONSTRAINT.
Hidden collisions retain E_RULE (and hidden write targets retain NOT_FOUND),
including their deliberately ambiguous public message: a rule denial, unreadable
key holder or physical authorization cut remain indistinguishable. The wording
says that a row you cannot read holds this key as one possible cause. Only the
developer-owned trace may identify the actual private clause. No new hidden
existence bit is exposed; this preserves LLP 3000 §7 and this RFC §5.

The engine/model/oracles preserve rollback, conflict order, null-key uniqueness,
if-absent behavior, device prediction and unchanged named refusal precedence.
Lean's visible secondary conflict code follows E_UNIQUE; explicit row projection
has the existing field semantics. Generated equivalent implicit/explicit sources,
Lean examples, simulator and both device stores exercise the projection and
unique conflict. Native and Lean fixed-seed runs must add no failure signatures.

A development-only check rejects an operation principal argument equal to a
bare declared persona (alice), naming dev:alice. Static principal column literals
get advisory N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL. Production HTTP does not perform the dev argument check: arbitrary account
principals remain valid and personas confer no authority. The static note is
a compilation advisory whenever `use personas` declares the matching name,
including a production schema that retains its development cast. Developer query/test
output may count observed physical rows filtered by their read rules through
an opt-in collector, outside the wire envelope and durable receipts. It never
adds hidden counts to serve, browser responses, sync, the device or output data;
counts describe encountered rows, not the whole unreadable population.

Every service-seat E_RULE path, durable or ephemeral, for an ordinary table denial, hidden collision
or authorization exhaustion appends the same static authored table rule clause.
It never identifies which cause occurred, or copies row values. This fulfils
the seat explanation without relaxing hidden-collision ambiguity.

//! Proved contradictions between caller gates and table rules; unknown is quiet.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — bounded, path-local contradiction proofs never infer that different roles or principals are disjoint.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{Diagnostic, ast::*};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

type Atom = (String, String, bool); // equality when true, inequality otherwise
// DNF: no alternatives is false; one empty alternative is unknown/true.
type Facts = Vec<Vec<Atom>>;
const LIMIT: usize = 64;
fn unknown() -> Facts {
    vec![vec![]]
}
fn combine(a: Facts, b: Facts, and: bool) -> Facts {
    if !and {
        return if a.len() + b.len() > LIMIT {
            unknown()
        } else {
            a.into_iter().chain(b).collect()
        };
    }
    if a.len().saturating_mul(b.len()) > LIMIT {
        return unknown();
    }
    a.into_iter()
        .flat_map(|a| b.iter().map(move |b| a.iter().chain(b).cloned().collect()))
        .collect()
}
fn possible(facts: &Facts) -> bool {
    facts.iter().any(|facts| {
        let mut roots = BTreeMap::<String, String>::new();
        fn root(roots: &BTreeMap<String, String>, term: &str) -> String {
            let mut at = term;
            while let Some(next) = roots.get(at) {
                at = next;
            }
            at.into()
        }
        for (a, b, equal) in facts {
            if !equal {
                continue;
            }
            let (a, b) = (root(&roots, a), root(&roots, b));
            if a != b {
                roots.insert(a, b);
            }
        }
        let mut literals = BTreeMap::new();
        for (a, b, _) in facts {
            for term in [a, b] {
                if term.starts_with("literal:") {
                    let r = root(&roots, term);
                    if let Some(old) = literals.insert(r, term)
                        && old != term
                    {
                        return false;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        facts
            .iter()
            .all(|(a, b, eq)| *eq || root(&roots, a) != root(&roots, b))
    })
}
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
struct Env {
    references: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    values: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    rows: BTreeMap<String, (String, BTreeMap<String, String>)>,
}
fn term(expr: &Expr, env: &Env, row: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>) -> Option<String> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Some("viewer".into()),
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => env
            .values
            .get(name)
            .cloned()
            .or_else(|| env.rows.get(name)?.1.get("id").cloned()),
        Expr::Literal {
            value: Literal::Number(text),
            site,
        } => Some(format!(
            "literal:Number({})",
            super::integer_literal(text, site).ok()?
        )),
        Expr::Literal { value, .. } => Some(format!("literal:{value:?}")),
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => match base.as_ref() {
            Expr::Focal { .. } => row?.get(name).cloned(),
            Expr::Name { name: alias, .. } => env.rows.get(alias)?.1.get(name).cloned(),
            _ => None,
        },
        _ => None,
    }
}
fn predicate(
    expr: &Expr,
    env: &Env,
    row: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>,
    positive: bool,
    table_rule: bool,
) -> Facts {
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. }
            if ["true", "false"].contains(&name.as_str())
                || table_rule && ["allow", "deny"].contains(&name.as_str()) =>
        {
            if (["allow", "true"].contains(&name.as_str())) == positive {
                unknown()
            } else {
                vec![]
            }
        }
        Expr::Literal {
            value: Literal::Bool(value),
            ..
        } => {
            if *value == positive {
                unknown()
            } else {
                vec![]
            }
        }
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => {
            predicate(value, env, row, !positive, table_rule)
        }
        Expr::Binary {
            op, left, right, ..
        } if op == "and" || op == "or" => combine(
            predicate(left, env, row, positive, table_rule),
            predicate(right, env, row, positive, table_rule),
            (op == "and") == positive,
        ),
        Expr::Binary {
            op, left, right, ..
        } if op == "=" || op == "!=" => match (term(left, env, row), term(right, env, row)) {
            (Some(a), Some(b)) => vec![vec![(a, b, (op == "=") == positive)]],
            _ => unknown(),
        },
        // Program probes are reads, whereas rule probes see hidden rows. Never
        // equate them or assume unrelated roster rows cannot overlap.
        _ => unknown(),
    }
}
